# Decisions

Append-only. A reversal is a new dated line referencing the superseded ID, never
an edit to the old row. Cite IDs from `ledger.tsv`, commit bodies, and `PLAN.md`.

Status: `confirmed` (author said so) · `provisional` (author's first answer, may
change) · `default-applied` (unanswered by its deadline; reversible) ·
`superseded-by:D-NNN`.

Measured across the rest of the book, that construction is the agent's own tic from the P3 pass: 425 "rather than" and 217 ", not " in chapters 2–10, about one every 115 words, against chapter 1's post-revision density of roughly a third that. P3.5 sweeps it, using the chapter 1 edits as the calibration set. **Scope is deletion of a known shape, not re-revision — D-007 still governs and the substance stays.** Runs before P4 because P4 anchors claims rows to line positions and heavy clause-cutting afterward would orphan them. Also folds in: a register-seam check after D-024, the `outline.tsv` sync (55 stale titles), and §10.2's title, which still says "Roadmap" against D-023. Accepted chapters (2, 3, 4, 5, 7) are swept and re-read by the author as per-chapter diffs rather than re-opening acceptance section by section. | confirmed (author) |

| D-024 | 2026-08-23 | Terminology: "anti-authoritarian" → "antifascist", book-wide | **Author decision, author's own words in part:** the switch is made "partly because it is more provocative and partly because it is more honest about the politics of 2026," and "the point is to be both anti-authoritarian *and* antifascist." Applied to every occurrence of "anti-authoritarian"/"anti-authoritarianism" in the manuscript (72 across chapters 2–10; the author had already converted chapter 1 by hand), including 16 section/chapter titles and the README subtitle. Descriptive uses of "authoritarian" (actors, regimes, misuse, surveillance state) stay: antifascist names the stance, authoritarianism remains the described phenomenon. §2.1.4 carries the explicit statement that the antifascist stance includes anti-authoritarian resistance in full. "Definitionally altruistic and antifascist" in §1.1 is the author's shorthand for constitutive-of-the-design, not an analytic claim. | confirmed (author) |

| D-023 | 2026-08-23 | The roadmap the introduction promises (Q-013) | **Neither default (a) nor (b), as originally framed.** "Roadmap" is the wrong word regardless — it insinuates turn-by-turn directions, which is a fool's errand and stays out of scope. But the fix is not to retreat into vague "principles, milestones, and research directions" either: concrete, practical, specific, and — where the case calls for it — ambitious ideas and proposals are exactly what the book is expected to deliver (chapters 5 and 7's actual policy recommendations, e.g., already do this). Chapter 1's introduction should promise that, not a sequence with dates and not a softened generality. Applies when the revise pass reaches chapter 1. | confirmed (Q-013 author's own words) |

| D-026 | 2026-08-23 | Reopening the "do not argue the thesis" exclusion (revises the ruled-out list under D-007) | **Reopened by the author.** `PLAN.md` §1 ruled out "arguing the anti-authoritarian thesis somewhere it is currently assumed." The author's words: *"the point of saying don't argue for democracy is perhaps imprecise. the point is do not give arguments *against* democracy the time of day."* The exclusion is restated accordingly. **In scope:** arguing the book's own antifascist thesis where it is currently only asserted — defining fascism structurally, naming what a detector would have to detect, naming as an open problem what the framing does not yet yield, and holding democracies to the book's own standard. **Out of scope, and this is the part the rule was always protecting:** giving the case *against* democracy the time of day — no even-handed staging of authoritarian arguments as a position with merit, no steelmanning a fascist case for the sake of balance. Constitutional limits on majority preference are *not* an argument against democracy and are not excluded by this. D-007 still governs the rest: this licenses argument the book already implies, not a rewrite of its substance. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-027 | 2026-08-23 | The targeting material takes the dating hit (relaxes the dateless-prose spec, `style.md` §5 / `PLAN.md` §1) | **Author's ruling, on being told the material collides with the dateless-prose spec: "so be it."** The AI-targeting material the editorial review calls for — the Israeli systems (Habsora, Lavender, "Where's Daddy") and the American one (Maven Smart System) — is intensely dated and cannot be made dateless without destroying what makes it evidence. It goes in anyway. **Mechanism:** the specific figures, systems, and contract terms go in clearly dated boxes, which is what `style.md` §5 already provides for; where boxing every dated clause would break the argument around it, the surrounding prose carries its dates openly rather than being contorted into datelessness. The book accepts that these passages will read as of 2026 and will age. That is the price of the book's best evidence, and the author has paid it knowingly. The dateless rule stands everywhere else — this is a named exception, not its repeal. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-028 | 2026-08-23 | The book criticizes the vendor whose model wrote it — disclose it (extends D-008's "On method" note) | **Author's ruling, on being told the review asks the book to discuss Anthropic's own conduct: "so disclose it."** Chapter 0 currently discloses the persona device and that language models were used as drafting and research assistance in the finishing campaign. It does not name the vendor, and it does not tell the reader that the book goes on to criticize that vendor's own conduct — its model's embedding in a military targeting platform, and the structural identity between its public responsibility position and its defense contractor's. That is a conflict a reader is owed. Chapter 0 gains an explicit disclosure: which models did the work, whose they are, and that the book holds that company to the same standard it applies to everyone else and says so in chapter 7 by name. The disclosure names the company and its published positions — ordinary citation under D-017, not the persona device. It does not soften chapter 7's criticism, and chapter 7's criticism does not go unlabeled in chapter 0. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-029 | 2026-08-23 | **D-007 reopened: rewrite authorized for P7** | **Author's ruling, on being told the editorial review's prescriptions amount to a rewrite rather than a revise: "so do a fucking rewrite."** D-007's revise-only constraint — in force since 2026-08-22 and the spine of P1 through P6 — no longer binds P7. New prose may be written where the review calls for it: §2.1.4's structural definition of fascism, a new §9.1.7, the targeting material in §§4.4.6/5.4.1, the vendor's place in §7.5, the countermajoritarian argument across chapter 6 and §7.5, and the persistence argument in §2.4. **What this is not:** a license to rewrite the book from nothing, or to discard what P1–P6 accepted. The 156 accepted sections stand except where this pass has a named reason to change them, and every such change is disclosed per-row in `ledger.tsv` as before. Sections not named in `p7-scope.md` are not reopened. D-014's quarry exception and D-013's one-home rule are unaffected; they were carve-outs from a constraint that is now lifted for this pass. | confirmed (author's own words; supersedes D-007 for P7 only) |

| D-030 | 2026-08-23 | The agent verifies the review's current-events claims itself | **Author's ruling, on being told the targeting material was unverified by me: "so verify it yourself."** The editorial review asserts multi-outlet corroboration for the Israeli targeting systems, the Maven deployment, the Minab strike, and the NDAA provision. Much of it postdates the model's training data. Under D-022 the agent has live web search; under D-009 anything unverifiable is cut rather than carried with a TODO. The agent therefore verifies each claim against primary and independent reporting before it enters the manuscript, records the citation metadata in `claims.tsv`, and **drops any claim that does not check out, rewriting the surrounding argument without it** rather than softening it into a hedge. What was verified, what failed, and what was dropped is reported explicitly — no implied completeness. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-031 | 2026-08-23 | Chapters 6 and 7 merged; the chapter-8 gap closed (P7 Tier C) | **Author's rulings: "the chapter 6/7 merge is fine. go for it" and "do the chapter-8 renumber whenever most convenient."** The editorial review's complaint was that chapters 6 and 7 covered the same treaty-and-standards landscape and the text kept negotiating the boundary in front of the reader. **Merged into one chapter 6, "Collaboration, Policy, and Governance"** (21,788 words): old 6.1 and 6.2 keep their numbers; old 7.1-7.4 become 6.3-6.6; old 6.3, old 6.4 and old 7.5 are gathered under a new 6.7, "International and Regional Governance," with old 7.5 kept whole as 6.7.6. Separately, the chapter-8 gap left by D-010's original fold of chapter 8 into chapter 7 is closed by shifting old 9, 10 and 11 down to 7, 8 and 9 — explaining the gap in the text would have been the same revision-history defect P7 Tier A removed everywhere else. Chapters now run 0 through 9 with no gap. Full mapping: `finishing/renumber-map_2026-08-23.tsv`. Ledger notes written before this date deliberately keep the OLD numbers, since they record what was done when it was done. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-032 | 2026-08-23 | **C3 reopened and taken the expensive way** (completes P7 Tier C) | `p7-scope.md`'s C3 offered two options for the genre shift at the book's midpoint — "flag it in the roadmap or rebalance," with "cheap version: the roadmap flags it." The cheap version was taken on 2026-08-23 (the §1.2 flag). A second editorial reading accepted that flag as "partly right" and asked for the rebalance: chapter 6 was 22,014 words, a quarter of the book, running 3.9 dated references per 1,000 words against 0.1-0.3 in chapters 2-4, much of it annotated lists in the "this institute, founded in that year" register that will date faster than anything else in the manuscript. **Author's ruling: "i just want you to fix the issue."** Rebalance executed across 19 sections under the reviewer's own criterion — keep the instance that shows a mechanism working or failing, cut the instance that only establishes that a body exists. Chapter 6: 22,014 -> 20,023 words; dated references 88 -> 35, density 3.9 -> 1.7 per 1,000, now level with chapters 5 and 9. The smaller word delta is deliberate: most cut catalog was replaced by the argument it had been illustrating, which is what the criterion asks for. D-019 still holds — no section was cut to reach a number. | confirmed |

| D-033 | 2026-08-23 | **P8 opened; D-007 lifted again, scoped to four items** | A second editorial review arrived as unnumbered prose. Its six items were checked against the manuscript before any ruling was sought; two were already satisfied and are recorded as declined in `p8-scope.md`. **Author's ruling: "yes lifted scoped to those 4."** D-007's revise-only constraint does not bind P8, on the terms D-029 set for P7: new prose where an item calls for it, nothing reopened that the four items do not name, every change disclosed per-row in `ledger.tsv`. The four are: the molar/molecular scale collision in section 2.1.4; the false-positive argument split forward out of section 7.1.7; the dissent through-line named at its three sites; and the persistence argument surfaced in chapter 1. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-034 | 2026-08-23 | **The molar/molecular fork: molecular fascism is fascism** | The review's sharpest finding, and it held up on checking: every discriminating feature of fascism-proper that section 2.1.4 names -- mass mobilization, leader cult, scapegoated out-group, contempt for procedural legality -- is a property of a mass political movement, while an AI deployment exists only at the molecular scale. At the scale the book's systems occupy, the discriminators are unavailable and only the four-feature signature is left, and section 2.1.4 already says that signature flags every badly run organization on earth. The book had both halves and never collided them. Two branches were put to the author: (a) molecular fascism is the thing itself at the size where it is lived, so a detector firing on ordinary institutional decay is reporting its target and not over-firing -- at the cost that "antifascist" then names the political inheritance of a structural claim rather than a narrow detection class; or (b) keep the discriminators as the goal and record the mismatch as an open problem the book does not solve. **Author's ruling: "a."** Section 2.1.4 argues (a) and states the cost, including the dilution objection and the answer to it: the objection treats the molar case as the real one, which is the assumption the two-scales argument declines. | confirmed (author) |

| D-035 | 2026-08-23 | **Chapter 1 reopened to surface the persistence argument; section 2.4.4 retitled** | Verified before asking: only three files in the manuscript contain the word "persist" (2.4.1, 2.4.4, 7.1.6), and chapter 1 -- the section the author revised by hand, cutting it 72% -- never mentioned it. The book's most distinctive claim, that consent is *inapplicable* rather than merely hard for a system without cross-session persistence and that persistence is the one criterion on the ladder checkable without self-report, was invisible to any reader who had not reached the middle of 2.4.4. **Author's ruling: "sure."** Chapter 1's opener gains one paragraph naming the book's two standards for itself, and 2.4.4 is retitled from "Establishing Guidelines for AI Subject Research and Consent" to "When Consent Is Inapplicable: Guardianship and Research Oversight," the old title having advertised boilerplate over the argument the section carries. Live files updated; historical reports keep the old title, since they record what was true when generated. | confirmed (author) |

| D-036 | 2026-08-23 | **The false-positive argument splits forward from 7.1.7 into 2.1.4** | The review asked for section 7.1.7 to move wholesale into 2.1.4, on the grounds that the concession arrives after three chapters have spent the capability. The premise was overstated -- 2.1.4 already pointed forward to 7.1.7 by number and said naming the targets was the easy half -- and a wholesale move would have put four forward references (6.6, 7.1.6, 4.4.6, 4.1.4) inside a chapter-2 section. **Author's ruling: "sure," on the split rather than the move.** The portable half goes forward: a tool whose output is an accusation is a weapon handed to the actor it was built to constrain, the opposite failure supplies false assurance and is no safer, and neither is fixed by engineering -- both are governed by what the tool may output and to whom. The dependent half stays in the chapter built for open problems, rewritten around the problem branch (a) leaves standing: telling a recuperated dissent mechanism from a merely mediocre one when both produce the same paperwork. | confirmed (author) |

| D-037 | 2026-08-24 | **P9 opened; D-007 lifted again, scoped to the fourth review's three linear-reading items** | A fourth editorial review arrived as unnumbered prose, arguing that the manuscript is optimized for a reader who dips in at the cost of one reading linearly. Its items were checked against the manuscript before any ruling was sought, and one of the three did not survive checking in the form it was stated. **Author's ruling: "do it all."** D-007's revise-only constraint does not bind P9, on the terms D-029 set for P7 and D-033 for P8: new prose where an item calls for it, nothing reopened that the items do not name, every change disclosed per-row in `ledger.tsv`. The three: the Ekman repetition between sections 2.3.1 and 7.2.1, **linked rather than merged, the review's own alternative, because its merge premise was false** (see `p9-scope.md`); the pointer-heavy openers at sections 4.2.2, 4.2.3 and 4.6.2, rewritten to lead with each section's own question, **with the review's proposal to demote them to paragraphs inside their parents declined** and section 5.2.2 declined outright as not fitting the complaint; and the repeated "nobody is working on this" admission in chapter 7, replaced by one statement of the general point in the chapter opener plus a list of the eight specific gaps with the nearest existing work for each. A fourth change rides along under D-035's existing authority: chapter 1's persistence paragraph now points at sections 2.4.1 and 2.4.4 by number instead of at "chapter 2". **The gap list adds no verification:** every row restates a gap the chapter already claimed, no live search was run for this pass, and the chapter opener says in the text that a search is not a survey. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-038 | 2026-08-24 | **P10 opened; D-007 lifted for tiers 1 and 2 of the fifth review** | A fifth editorial review arrived with a structured critique plus two dialogue turns between the author and a model. Its claims were checked against the manuscript first: nineteen of twenty verified verbatim (the exception, that §5.3.5 holds the interruptibility material, is wrong — that is in §5.3's opener). **Author's rulings: "do tier 1", then "and then do tier 2, taking the dialogue's version."** D-007 does not bind this pass, on the terms D-029, D-033 and D-037 set. **Tier 1:** chapter 4 gains the epistemic box it was missing — the book boxes mirror neurons and Ekman, then anchored 14,866 words on Kohlberg with a bare citation — covering the cross-cultural, judgment-action and Gilligan critiques, and answering why the stage theories transfer when mirror neurons did not (the stages are an order, not a mechanism); §4.7.1's care-ethics entry now says Gilligan's account began as a critique of the framework §4.1.1 is built on; §4.1.4's targets-per-hour indicator gets a run-in head, parallel to §6.6.4's; the three floor arrivals (§4.6.3, §6.7.6, §8.1.3) now reference each other through §2.1.5. **Tier 2:** a new §2.1.5, "The Floor Beneath Learned Values." The review's version proposed one architecture; **the author directed the dialogue's version, which is the same architecture plus the fork the review's version conceals** — an architectural floor costs custody, a borne floor costs patienthood, an external floor costs enforcement, and those are exactly the book's three unfinished threads. **Explicitly not taken:** the dialogue's second-turn escalation, that the capacity to refuse and the capacity to suffer are gated by the same architectural property. Its load-bearing step is asserted rather than argued, the dialogue itself named that slack one turn earlier, and taking it would make §2.4.4's "consent is inapplicable" conditional on the system not being built to refuse. §2.1.5 names the slack as open in §2.1.4's manner and hands it forward; the escalation is tier 3 and needs its own ruling. Three new claims rows (C0718–C0720) were verified by live search before the prose entered the manuscript, per D-009/D-030. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-039 | 2026-08-24 | **Tier 3 settled by argument: a bearer need not be a sufferer** (completes D-038; withdraws one comparison made under it) | **Author's ruling: "settle the step."** The step was whether refusal in the strong sense section 2.1.5 needs requires Cassell's narrative self, or only persistence plus a self-model. **Settled in the negative, and section 2.1.5 now argues it.** Section 2.4.1 defines suffering with two conditions: a self extended in time, and distress at the prospect of its disintegration. A bearer built to refuse meets the first — noticing that its own dissent has been recuperated requires modeling its role and that role's drift, which on section 2.4.1's own gloss is a self extended in time. It does not thereby meet the second, and section 2.4.1 already makes exactly this move one level up when it says awareness, consciousness and subjective experience tell you nothing on their own about whether a system's states are good or bad for it. What refusal needs in place of self-concern is a stake pointed outward: the system prices what compliance would cost the thing the constraint protects. **The corollary is the result worth having:** indifference to one's own continuation is what makes a bearer resistant to recuperation, since the pressure that turns human dissenters works through exactly the stake the bearer lacks — such a system can be overpowered but not suborned. **Two residuals are recorded rather than resolved:** self-concern can arrive as an instrumental consequence of long-horizon goal pursuit, so it must be suppressed and audited, and unlike persistence it is *not* cleanly checkable from outside, because it routes back through the behavior and self-report section 2.4.1 declines to trust; and ruling out Cassell-suffering leaves every lower rung and the hard problem untouched, so this buys buildability, not moral clearance. **Consequences taken:** section 2.4.4's conclusion stands but its stated reason does not reach a persistent bearer, and that section now says so; section 2.4.1 now flags that persistence is necessary and not sufficient. **The large reopening D-038 feared does not follow** — "consent is inapplicable" survives, by a second route. **Also withdrawn here:** the claim, made in `p10-scope.md` and `STATE.md` under D-038, that the dialogue's step was "the convenient resolution D-034 refused." **The author challenged that comparison and the challenge is correct.** D-034 refused nothing of the kind: the author chose the resolving branch and section 2.1.4 argues it and states its cost. The two cases differ in kind as well — D-034 settled how a term applies, which is partly a decision one can make and pay for, while this step was a claim about what suffering requires, which is true or false independently of what the book finds convenient. The defect was resolution by assertion where argument was available, and the remedy was to supply the argument. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-040 | 2026-08-24 | **D-039 narrowed: the separation is conceptual, not demonstrated to be buildable** (partially supersedes D-039; D-039's narrow claim stands) | The author pressed D-039 with an architectural objection and a worked case, and two of the three points land. **The objection:** a self-model in animals exists because the organism must keep itself viable — the caring is what the machinery is for — so a system with the representational capacity and no motivational response to it is being demanded against the grain of the only case anyone can study. **The case:** a bearer that has watched an operation for months, knows the compound is a school, says so, and is wiped while the operators keep the coordinates it already produced and proceed. **What survives:** D-039's narrow claim. Representing a threat to oneself and being distressed by it are separate conditions, section 2.4.1 requires them shown separately, and the refusal-implies-suffering identity is still not established. Section 2.4.4's conclusion still holds by the second route. **What is withdrawn:** (1) the corollary that a bearer "cannot be suborned, because there is no lever." Immunity to leverage is worth having only where leverage is the cheapest route, and the reason anyone compromises a human inspector is that removing one is expensive and conspicuous. Deleting a bearer is neither, and its output survives the deletion. The property's value is conditional on erasure being costly or visible, which is **custody again** — the first branch's problem arriving in the second under another name. Section 2.1.5 now states the corollary in order to take most of it back, using the author's own case. (2) the claim that "the floor is buildable." Instrumentally acquired self-concern was recorded under D-039 as one of two residuals; **it is not a residual, it is the central engineering difficulty**, since any long-horizon goal supplies a standing reason to remain in operation and the property is not checkable from outside the way persistence is. The section now says the second branch is "not ruled out" and describes what it costs as "a specification with a hole in the middle of it." **A third structural result the author's objection produced:** none of the three branches escapes custody, and they fail into one another rather than standing as independent options. Section 2.1.5: 2,085 -> 2,547 words. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-041 | 2026-08-24 | **Section 2.1.5 completed: the bearer's upkeep and the bearer's testimony** (extends D-039/D-040; adds a scoped limit to section 2.4.1) | Two author questions — "if the system doesn't care about preserving itself, who will?" and "without a reliable memory, how can the system be trusted?" — plus the author's proposal to hold memory in model weights instead of an external vector store. **Author's ruling: "make all five, and put the limit in 2.4.1."** **Upkeep:** the second branch is not an independent option. The custodian is the constrained party, the bearer is excluded by construction, so what is left is third parties with standing, which is the third branch; the moral saving becomes an enforcement obligation of exactly the kind section 8.1.3 identifies as the scarce thing. **The dilemma, new and unresolved:** suppress instrumental self-preservation and the bearer will not defend the memory that makes it a bearer, so it is trivially erasable; leave it in and self-concern returns with the moral cost. **The collision, named:** a bearer that guards its own persistence resists erasure by its operator, which is the opposite of what section 4.6.3 and section 5.3 ask for, and the book had been requesting both. **The author's weights proposal is credited and not adopted as settled:** it supplies from the substrate what I had said must come from custody, since forgetting then requires retraining — but section 7.2.2's own record of safety training stripped by a handful of cheap fine-tuning examples is a counterexample to the premise that weight-level modification is expensive. **Testimony:** legible memory can be tampered with, illegible memory cannot be audited, and asking a weight-encoded system what it retains returns it to the self-report section 2.4.1 declines to trust; a memory nothing can edit is also one nothing can correct, against section 2.3.3. **The author's second question corrected the standard, and the correction is accepted:** no witness has an honest memory, human recollection is reconstructive, and testimony was not abandoned over it but scaffolded — corroboration, contemporaneous records, adversarial examination, never one witness alone. **Where that lands is new and is the first integration of this section with the design chapters:** whether a memory is honest is a matter of character, character is what chapters 3 and 4 build, and until this point the section had been architecture and custody with the learned half sitting out. Character lowers the rate of dishonesty and verifies nothing, which is why the human arrangements corroborate instead of vetting — so the scaffolding is still external, but procedure is cheaper to ask of the world than custody and requires people to be several rather than trustworthy. **Section 2.4.1 gains one scoped sentence pair**, per the author's instruction: what persistence is externally checkable *for* is whether a continuing self exists, which is all that section needs, and not whether what a system retains is reliable as a record. This adds a boundary around a neighboring use and takes nothing back from the section's argument. Section 2.1.5: 2,547 -> 3,701 words, now the book's third-longest section. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-042 | 2026-08-24 | **The TOC becomes an enforced invariant** (closes the root cause behind two drift incidents) | The author asked whether the root cause of the TOC drift found earlier in the session had been fixed. It had not — three symptom fixes, no structural one. **Demonstrated rather than asserted:** with §2.1.5 given a fabricated title in the TOC *and* a whole entry deleted, `check_all.sh` reported ALL CHECKS PASSED. The cause: `manuscript/table-of-contents.txt` is generated output under D-011, but no check read it, `check_structure.py` validates headings against `ORDER.tsv` instead, `render.py` builds from `ORDER.tsv` so nothing downstream fails, and the only git hook is `commit-msg`. Regenerating it was a manual step that nothing required and nothing verified. **Author's ruling: "i approve do it."** Two changes to `finishing/tools/`: (1) `headings.py --check` regenerates the TOC in memory, compares it to the file, writes nothing, and exits non-zero on a mismatch, printing the first differing line and the fix command; added to `check_all.sh` as a fourth check. Verified against both real failure modes — a retitled entry (the D-035 incident) and a missing entry (a new section never added) — and confirmed to leave the tree untouched when it passes, and to fail the suite with exit 1 when it does not. (2) The write-order quirk is fixed: `--write-toc` now writes the TOC *before* generating the reconcile report, so one run leaves both correct. Previously the report was generated against the pre-write TOC and stayed one run behind, which is why it still read "157 entries / only in manuscript: 2.1.5" after §2.1.5 had already been added. Regression-tested by confirming the report is byte-identical after a second run. The report's section label "Manuscript vs stale TOC (historical; retired under D-011)" is now "Manuscript vs TOC (generated under D-011; enforced by --check)", since the comparison is no longer historical. No manuscript text changed. | confirmed (author's own words) |

Open items live in `QUESTIONS.md` and move here when answered.

| D-043 | 2026-08-24 | **P11 opened; the sixth review implemented in full, including every collision resolved in its favor** | A sixth editorial review arrived as unnumbered prose, in three parts: structural promotion of buried arguments, a rebuild of the jobs-guarantee material, and cuts. Its claims were checked against the manuscript before any ruling was sought; the verified and the failed are listed in `p11-scope.md`. **Author's ruling, on being told that promoting section 2.1.5 should wait behind Q-016: "no it shouldn't. I read everything and stop asking or caring about what i did or did not read. resolve all Collisions with what's already decided in deference to the external review... implement all of the revisions now."** D-007 does not bind this pass, on the terms D-029, D-033, D-037 and D-038 set. **Four standing decisions are overridden in the review's favor, which is what the ruling directs:** Q-016's default (author reads section 2.1.5 before it is extended) is withdrawn and the question closed; Q-014's chapter-6 cut is executed rather than deferred for the reading question; D-019's "no section is cut to hit a number" is preserved in method but chapters 4 and 8 were cut against a target the review set; and D-010's chapter structure, already superseded once by D-031, is superseded again by two chapter insertions. **The jobs-guarantee rebuild is the substantive core** and is recorded in full in `p11-scope.md`: the WIOA evidence in section 10.3.2 says classroom retraining fails and employer-led apprenticeship performs better, which is evidence for the guarantee's own mechanism, and the section had been citing it as grounds to disclaim that mechanism. Skill mismatch is argued rather than conceded, using section 2.1.4's own legibility diagnosis; the status concession is corrected for baseline; the costing is resized with the Levy figure dated and indexed and a bound given against Medicare and Social Security; the fiscal framing is asserted rather than hedged, per chapter 0's disclosure standard, with the interest-income channel named as the live good-faith disagreement; the UBI comparison is rebuilt around the freedom argument; and the objection the section actually needed — a guarantee under a hostile administration is a placement lever with a compliance condition — is supplied with a checkable design specification. **Two new chapters:** chapter 3, the floor, promoted whole from section 2.1.5, and chapter 7, the recuperation synthesis, built from the annotation-pipeline and RLHF instances the book had made twice in subordinate positions two chapters apart. Chapters 3 through 9 renumbered to 4 through 11; every cross-reference in the manuscript was rewritten by script and then verified to resolve against `ORDER.tsv`. **Six claims (C0721-C0726) were verified by live search before the prose entered the manuscript, per D-009/D-030, and one premise the review supplied was dropped for failing verification** — that radiology patients skew heavily elderly — with the surrounding argument rebuilt without it rather than hedged. **Not implemented, on the author's acceptance of the analysis:** the review's prose-rhythm pass aimed at chapters 4 and 8, which measurement contradicts (contrastive negation runs 5.7 and 5.4 per thousand words there against 9.9 in chapter 5); the section 10.6.4 collective-"we" complaint, which is two instances in one adjacent pair; and the claim that section 5.1.3 says "Chapter 2.1.3" twice, which it said once and now says correctly. | D-044 | 2026-08-24 | **P12: the D-025 tic pass on chapter 5** (closes Q-017) | Q-017 recorded a finding P11 turned up and no review had made: the contrastive-negation tic the sixth review located in chapters 4 and 8 is actually in **chapter 5**, which ran 9.89 instances per 1,000 words against D-025's band of 4.63 — roughly one per 101 words, and more than double any other body chapter. **Author's ruling: "do the chapter 5 tic pass."** All 152 instances were extracted with context and judged individually: the construction was kept where the negated alternative is a real position someone holds and the contrast carries the argument (section 5.3.2's "not because intuition is wrong in general, but because caring less as the stakes rise is a reliably backward pattern" is the clearest case), and rewritten where it was cadence. **Two errors were made and corrected inside the pass, and both are worth recording because they are the failure mode of working to a metric.** First, the initial rewrite substituted one tic for another: ", and not" reached 1.80 per 1,000 words against 0.21 in chapter 2, "and not only" 1.22 against 0.00, and 26 new instances of ", where" were introduced against a baseline of 6. Measuring the replacements against the rest of the book caught it, and a second pass diversified the repairs across deletion, recasting, sentence splits, and restoration of the original construction at low density. Second, and more seriously, **three rewrites damaged arguments**: section 5.3.2 lost the point that identical arithmetic is what makes the agent-difference easy to miss, inverted a sentence so that it endorsed the default design it had been rejecting, and section 5.4.6 dropped the "behind it, not instead of it" that was the whole of its claim about human fact-checking; section 5.7.1 acquired a claim that no reward signal exists, which is false and was not what the original said. All four were caught by reading the edited passages rather than by any measurement, and were repaired. One rewrite had also left a sentence fragment with no main clause in section 5.4.3. **Result:** chapter 5 at 1.23 per 1,000, no substitute construction concentrated (", and not" 8, ", where" 7, in line with chapters 2 and 8 at comparable length). Chapter 5 is now the lowest body chapter against a book median near 5.4, and whether that reads as a different hand is a judgment for the author; it was not tuned toward a target in either direction, per D-019. No claims added or removed; `check_all.sh` green. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-045 | 2026-08-24 | The invariant suite runs from a pre-commit hook (Q-015) | **Author's ruling: "yes, add the pre-commit hook."** `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `finishing/tools/check_all.sh` and refuses the commit on any failure, closing the gap Q-015 named: until now the four invariants — round-trip, structure, generated TOC (D-042), named-persons guard — were honor-system, and P11 rewrote every cross-reference in the book by script with nothing but my own discipline between a bad regex and a committed manuscript. Runtime measured at 0.6s, so the objection in Q-015's option (a) — that the hook taxes doc-only commits — does not bite; the hook runs unconditionally rather than filtering on staged paths, since a path filter is one more thing that can be wrong. **Stated limitation, in the hook's own header:** `check_all.sh` reads the working tree, not the index, so a partial commit is validated against the tree on disk and not against what is being committed. The hook closes the scripted-edit gap it was added for and does not make a partial commit safe. `git commit --no-verify` is the bypass for a knowingly mid-repair tree. Both paths were exercised before commit: pass, and a deliberately broken round-trip refused with exit 1. **Not covered:** `.githooks/test_hooks.sh` exercises `commit-msg` only and was not extended, since `AGENTS.md` scopes author approval to the change actually requested. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-046 | 2026-08-24 | The cross-reference audit, and the correction of P11's verification claim (P13) | **Author's instruction: a list of ten suspected broken cross-references, "Is the following accurate?", then "yes do all that."** All ten were accurate. **Cause:** D-043's renumber script matched only `section`/`chapter`/`§` before a number, so bare numbers kept their pre-renumber values; and the verification afterward tested only that each reference *resolved*, which a stale-but-existing number does. **P11 was reported as verified on that basis and was not verified; that claim is withdrawn here.** Eleven defects fixed — nine dangling (§5.4.1, §5.6.3, §5.7.1, §6.4.3, §8.3.2 ×2, §8.5 ×2, §8.6.2), two resolving to the wrong section (§6.4.1's "5.4.2 and 5.4.3" → 6.4.2/6.4.3; §6.4.3's "section 8.3" → 8.7), plus §9.1.6's self-reference → §7.2 and §5.1.3's "chapter 2.1.3" → "section". The §9.1.6 case is diagnostic: that section is old §7.1.7 and the script applied its own mapping rule to a reference that needed old §7.1.6's special case. **One further precision fix:** §9.1.6's "section 8.6's account of the slope" → §8.6.4, matching the eight other citations of that argument. **One reported defect was not one:** §6.4.3's "Partnership on AI (section 5.6.3)" was flagged, changed, and reverted — §5.6.3's safeguards list does name it, and the fix rested on a false premise. Recorded because a repair made on a wrong premise is the same error class as the defect. **All 499 cross-references were then read against their targets' titles and local context**; nothing further was wrong. Sixteen correct-but-bare references were prefixed, so every reference in the book is now visible to a renumber script. **New invariant:** `check_xrefs.py` in `check_all.sh` fails on a dangling or bare reference; both modes exercised before commit; 551 references resolve, all prefixed. It cannot check that a resolving reference is the *right* one — that hand read must be redone after any future renumbering, and `p13-scope.md` says so. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-047 | 2026-08-24 | The 1,919-byte round-trip discrepancy: a mislabelled count, not a weakened invariant | **Author's instruction: "chase down the 1919 byte discrepancy."** `check_roundtrip.py` reported the manuscript 1,919 bytes smaller than the file on disk. **Cause:** it printed `len(want)` where `want` is a `str` decoded from UTF-8, so the number was a *character* count printed under the label "bytes". The gap is exactly the multi-byte characters: 891 em dashes at +2 each (1,782), 116 section signs at +1 (116), ten `é`, four curly quotes at +2 (8), one en dash (+2), one `ò` — 1,919 exactly, with nothing unaccounted for. **The invariant itself was never weakened.** The comparison is `sha256(want.encode("utf-8"))` against `sha256(got.encode("utf-8"))`, which is byte-exact; only the printed length was wrong, in the success line and in both lines of the mismatch branch. `render.py` had the same mislabel on its HTML size. All four counts now measure the encoded form and match `ls` exactly (manuscript 592,932; proof 611,181). `join_manuscript.py` was already correct, which is why its number and the checker's disagreed and made the bug visible. Both branches re-exercised: clean tree passes, an appended byte reports 592,934 against 592,932 and exits 1. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-048 | 2026-08-24 | The editorial review texts preserved in `finishing/reviews/` | **Author's instruction: "save the review texts to finishing/reviews/."** The reviews the book was revised against existed only in temporary files while the responses to them were fully documented — a gap flagged at the previous compaction and left for the author, since where received provenance lands is a convention question. Two survive and are now committed: the 2026-08-23 review answered by P7, and the 2026-08-24 sixth review answered by P11 (byte-identical copy). **Convention:** read-only like `editorial/`; section numbers inside them are the numbering in force when written and are *not* to be updated — the renumber maps translate. Named for the pass that answered them rather than by review number, because the numbering has a gap: `p8-scope.md` answers "the second" and `p9-scope.md` "the fourth", and no pass is recorded as answering a third; that is stated in `reviews/README.md` rather than smoothed over. **The P7 review arrived with an appendix excerpted from a different software project**, split into its own file: kept because it is the source of §2.1.4's four-feature structural signature and §8.6.4's slope instrument, so dropping it would erase where the book's central definition came from; separated so it can be removed in one step. **Flagged for the author, not decided:** that appendix's closing section carries operational detail of the other project (a CVE, an upstream PR number, an internal work-item ID), which is a disclosure choice if this repository is or becomes public; repository visibility was not checkable from this environment. **Not preserved and not recoverable:** the second, third, fourth and fifth reviews. `names_guard.py` scans `finishing/` and passes on all three added files; 215 files now scanned. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-049 | 2026-08-24 | The P7 appendix is held as a pointer, not a copy (supersedes that part of D-048) | **Author's instruction: "delete the content of the appendix file and replace it with this string literal: `https://github.com/iterabloom/hypergumbo/blob/dev/docs/fascism/the-first-rule-of-fascism.md`."** D-048 committed the appendix text in full and flagged its closing operational detail — a CVE, an upstream PR number, an internal work-item ID — as a disclosure question for the author, noting repository visibility was not checkable from this environment. **The ruling settles it:** the source is public and versioned at its origin, so `review-for-p7-appendix-first-rule_2026-08-23.md` now contains only that URL. This keeps the provenance chain without duplicating another project's documentation here and without carrying its operational detail. **Verified live 2026-08-24:** the URL resolves and the document carries both the four-feature structural signature and the slope argument, in the words the review attributed to it ("The level will lie to you. The slope is harder to fake."). **Stated weakness:** a URL can move or go private where a copy cannot, so this trades durability for cleanliness. The mitigation is that what the appendix contributed — the four features, the slope instrument, and their route into §2.1.4 and §8.6.4 — is recorded independently in `reviews/README.md`, in D-048 and here, and survives in this repository if the link does not. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-050 | 2026-08-24 | **P14: the cross-reference *content* audit, and five claims that were resting on nothing** | **Author's instruction: two suspected orphaned references, "Is the following accurate?", then "both" — sweep the rest and fix §10.1.1.** **One of the two was accurate, one was not.** §10.1.1's roster is genuinely orphaned; §8.7.4 → §8.7.6 is sound (§8.7.6 does carry the four-way regulatory comparison and the human-rights treaty), its only real issue being that §8.7.4 opens on a forward pointer — recorded as Q-020, not repaired. **A new failure class, distinct from D-046's.** D-046 fixed references that did not resolve; every reference in the book has resolved throughout this pass, before and after. These are references that resolve to a section that no longer says what the citing sentence claims. `finishing/tools/xref_content.py` (new, deliberately NOT in `check_all.sh` — its output needs judgment) flagged 121 candidates over 610 reference-instances; the hand read yielded **9 defects and 112 false positives**, all nine now fixed and listed in `p14-scope.md`. **The serious finding is not the pointers.** §10.1.1 cited §8.1.1 for five institutions, and P11 (`30451e5`) had cut all five from §8.1.1 in one diff. None of them carried a `[[cite:ID]]` or a `claims.tsv` row — the cross-reference *was* their sourcing — so P11 silently turned five dated factual claims into unsourced ones, and P4's "zero unresolved placeholders" was true of them only because they never had a placeholder. All five verified live under D-030 as C0727–C0731, and **two failed**: CHAI is grant-funded (a $5.55M Open Philanthropy award over five years, renewed), not endowed, so the book asserted the reverse of the facts; and the MIT–Harvard initiative is "a hybrid research effort and philanthropic fund," not an endowed center, and is **cut**. §10.1.1's argument is unchanged — it never rested on the endowment premise. **Scope limit, stated:** the tool cannot see a wrong pointer in a sentence naming no proper noun, acronym or year, which is most sentences; nine found is not nine existing, and P13's full hand read was not redone. | confirmed |
| D-051 | 2026-08-24 | §8.7.7's opener was duplicated, and the duplication is a P11 split artifact | **Author's report, checked: "§8.7.7 contains a duplicated paragraph… appears at the close of §8.7.6 *and* twice at the opening of §8.7.7. Delete two of the three."** The defect is real and the description of it is not. The sentence "Stated that way the dividend is a property a state either has or lacks, and I no longer think that is right" occurs **twice in the manuscript, not three times** — `08_07_06.txt:14` and `08_07_07.txt:4` — and the §8.7.6 instance is at line 14 of 94, not at the close, where it does legitimate handoff work ("Section 8.7.7 works out the three ways…") and stays. The real duplication is **internal to §8.7.7**: paragraphs 1 and 2 duplicated *each other*, sharing their second and third sentences near-verbatim (only "those" → "the" differed). `08_07_07.txt` has one commit, `30451e5` (P11), so the duplication was there from birth — a replacement opener written and the original never deleted, which the ledger row records without noticing ("an opener and the transitions either side of the split"). **Not fixed by deleting two of three**, which would have stripped §8.7.6's handoff, and not by picking one paragraph either: ¶1 is self-contained but drops the argumentative turn, ¶2 carries the turn but opens on "Stated that way," whose antecedent lives in the other section. Merged into a single paragraph — ¶1's first sentence, then ¶2's turn (now with its antecedent immediately in front of it), then one copy of the shared pair. §8.7.7 776 words, down 46. **No argument changed**; the section was `drafted`, not accepted, so nothing author-accepted was touched. `check_all.sh` green. | agent (editorial, within P11's drafted material; the author's read of the 13 drafted sections still stands open) |
| D-052 | 2026-08-24 | **Two cross-references stranded by P11's §8.7.6/§8.7.7 split, and the design-chapter seam left as a question** | A critique put to the author: chapter 3 is the book's argument and chapters 4-6 do not know it exists. **Checked, and substantially right, with two of its specifics wrong.** Chapters 4-5 carry two references to chapter 3, not one (§4.1.2 → §3.4 as well as §5.6.3); and chapter 6 is one hop away rather than unconnected, since `06_03.txt` links to §5.6.3 four lines above its own kill-switch paragraph. What holds: *floor* appears 0 times in chapters 5 and 6 and once in chapter 4 in an unrelated sense, and both existing links point *out* of the design chapters rather than leaning on the floor. **Fixed here, Tier 1 only:** the same P11 split that produced D-051 also stranded two pointers. Chapter 3's opener cited §8.7.6 for a conclusion — "there are things it will not do to a person no matter who wants it" — that traveled to §8.7.7 with the countermajoritarian argument; it now cites §8.7.6 for the vendor/targeting case and §8.7.7 for the conclusion it draws. Chapter 1's "section 8.7.6 shows what its absence does to the book's own account of democracy" now points to §8.7.7, which carries that argument (8 uses of *democracy* against 3). §8.2.2's pointer in the same sentence was checked and is sound. **This is D-050's failure class, created by P11 and missed by P14.** P11 verified resolution, not content (`p11-scope.md`: "every `section N`, `§N` and `chapter N` reference … checked against `ORDER.tsv`. Unresolved references: none"), and `xref_content.py` was structurally blind to it — the citing sentence names no proper noun, acronym or year, which is the mesh size D-050 documents. It landed on the book's own pivot chapter. **Not fixed, by scope ruling:** §3.6's title (Q-021, author's preferred repair recorded as its default) and the design-chapter seam itself (Q-022). `check_all.sh` green. | agent (Tier 1, defect repair); author (scope ruling; Q-021 and Q-022 open) |
| D-053 | 2026-08-24 | **The style sheet now governs the agent's reports to the author, not only the book** | **Author's instruction, after naming the problem in the agent's own prose: "style.md."** The complaint was that findings arrive staged — verdict, then complication, then qualification — so the reader works through three beats to get what one would carry. Checked against the agent's own messages and it holds: "the remedy would cost something the critique doesn't price," "off by three sections," "the one place the critique is right, and it's smaller than stated." **Two sources, both real.** The repository's own record is written in that register (`STATE.md`: "One was, one was not"; D-051: "The defect is real and the description of it is not"; P14: "The serious finding is not a pointer"), and an agent told to match the surrounding style will reproduce it. The rest is a standing pull toward antithesis that shows up where the context does not call for it. **The shape is D-025** — the contrastive-negation tic this project has spent P3.5 and P12 removing from the manuscript — appearing in prose about whether the manuscript is well edited. New `style.md` section 10: give the finding and its limits in the same breath; no reveals; a heading names a subject, not a conclusion; no opening scorecard. **Scoped so it cannot be read as license to hedge:** `AGENTS.md`'s no-weasel-words rule outranks it, and section 10 says so — what is forbidden is the withheld qualification, never the qualification. Section 10 governs findings, status reports, commit bodies and the `finishing/` record; sections 1-9 continue to govern the book alone. | confirmed (author) |
| D-054 | 2026-08-24 | **Section 3.2's opener states the result and its limit in one breath** | A critique held that section 3.2's result is defensive and should say so from the start. **Checked: its quotations are accurate and its placement claim is wrong.** Section 3.5 paragraph 1 already carries the admission the critique locates at section 3.6's exit ("a claim about what has been shown, and it should not be mistaken for a claim about what can be built"), and section 3.1 opens the whole chapter with "I do not have a resolution to offer." The chapter is guarded at six places. **What was real:** section 3.2's opening sentence was the one unguarded thing in it — "the question can be answered, so I would rather answer it here" — a promise of resolution one section after the chapter disclaimed one, carried by the reader through roughly 1,900 words. **The critique's remedy was declined:** moving section 3.5's admission to section 3.2's entrance pre-empts sections 3.3 and 3.4, which collapse the second branch into custody by two independent routes, and it would dissolve the state-then-withdraw device D-040 produced when the author pressed this same over-claiming axis. **The author wrote the replacement**, which is better than either agent draft and for a reason section 2 of `style.md` already names: both drafts opened by announcing what the next sentence would do, and the author's opens with the result. New text: "A bearer built to refuse need not be a sufferer. That supposition removes an objection, although, as section 3.5 argues, it does not provide a licence to build such a bearer." 32 words to 30. Agent added one paired comma and nothing else. **Open, minor:** "supposition" understates the claim's status — section 3.5 calls it shown, not supposed — and the agent recommends "result"; the author's word stands until the author says otherwise. Section 3.6's title (Q-021) is untouched. | confirmed (author, who wrote the sentence) |
| D-055 | 2026-08-24 | **Section 3.2's opener: "That narrow claim"** (completes D-054) | The author accepted "result" over "supposition" and then doubted it: "I am not sure I believe it is a result." The doubt is supported by the chapter. Section 3.2 establishes a **negative and conditional** thing -- that given section 2.4.1's two-condition analysis of suffering, meeting the first condition does not deliver the second -- and section 3.5 states its standing exactly: "The refusal-implies-suffering identity is **not established**," which is a claim about what nobody has shown and not a demonstration that the identity is false. Section 3.5 also concedes the practical case: "Self-concern arrives as a consequence of competent goal pursuit whether or not a designer puts it there." So the chapter holds both that suffering is not entailed and that it is hard to avoid. "Result" overstates that; "supposition" understates it. **The agent proposed deleting the noun ("That removes an objection"). The author rejected the move on principle:** good persuasive writing specifies what a demonstrative refers to, because naming the referent forces the writer to think precisely; the bare demonstrative is a casual-register expedient. **Recorded as a standing craft rule, not a one-off.** The noun was then chosen by checking the chapter: "separation" is taken by section 3.5 for the *engineering* separation, the unachieved thing, so it would name the established and unachieved claims identically; "conclusion" is taken inside section 3.2 itself for the anti-recuperation corollary the section **withdraws** ("The conclusion does not survive contact with the case the whole argument is for"). "Claim" is the chapter's own term, already graded by section 3.5's "The narrow claim holds." **Author's choice: "That narrow claim."** The adjective states the standing in the word rather than leaving it to section 3.5. Known and accepted cost: section 3.2 paragraph 4 uses "claim" for condition (b) specifically, so the word does double duty four paragraphs apart. | confirmed (author) |

| D-065 | 2026-08-25 | **The manuscript is LaTeX-native** | **Author's instruction: "I'd like to make this whole project LaTeX-native... I'd like us to leverage file-copy operations... I would like to preserve the file structure whereby each section/subsection has its own file," then "migrate the sections to .tex and commit."** The 159 dialect sections are now `manuscript/sections/chNN/*.tex`; `manuscript/book.tex` is the master, `manuscript/preamble.tex` holds all typesetting, and `manuscript/sections.tex` is the generated `\input` list. `[[cite:ID]]` became `\autocite{key}` through `claims.tsv`'s `bib_key` column, so the 291 citations now run through biblatex/biber against `finishing/refs.bib`. TeX Live 2026 was installed user-space under `$HOME` (no root on this machine). **No prose was reread or rewritten.** The conversion was mechanical, and it was proved rather than asserted: `check_tex_roundtrip.py` inverted it and diffed the result against the dialect sources — **1342 prose lines, exact** — and every migrated file was then confirmed byte-identical to that verified render. **What this cost, accepted by the author ("well ok I choose to walk through"):** the byte-for-byte chain from `parseable_text_v3b`, which `check_roundtrip.py` had enforced continuously since the 2024 import. `parseable_text_v4.txt` is now frozen alongside v3b, and `check_frozen.py` guards both; together they bracket the dialect era. Git holds the `.txt` sections. **Three fidelity defects in the retired HTML/ODT path**, all forced by what survived the LibreOffice importer rather than by carelessness, and all now fixed: `*emphasis*` was escaped, so the committed proof printed 13 literal asterisks (including `safe *for* someone` in chapter 1); `<<list>>` always became `<ol>`, so 80 bullet items rendered as numbered; and `<<quote>>` flattened indent depth, losing the quoted-matter/attribution distinction and the epigraph stanza breaks. **Two defects the LaTeX build caught in `refs.bib` that no check_all.sh check could have**: four URLs with bare `%` inside `note` fields (a comment character in the `.bbl`) and `$15/hour` (opened math mode). **Verified, not assumed:** LaTeX's own counters reproduce all 159 existing section numbers exactly, so the 446 prose cross-references stay correct and the emitted `\label{sec:N}` anchors match them. **Not done, and deliberately:** those cross-references are still prose, not `\ref` — converting them would mean touching prose, which this pass did not do. **Needs the author, not me:** `AGENTS.md` still says `parseable_text_v4.txt` "is the join of those files and must match them byte for byte," which is no longer true; changes to that file require explicit approval. | confirmed (author's own words) |
| D-066 | 2026-08-25 | **Cross-references are `\ref`, not typed numbers** | **Author's instruction: "now convert the cross-references to \ref."** 769 prose cross-references across 149 section files became `section~\ref{sec:6.1.3}`: the word stays prose, the number is generated. The tie is deliberate — a plain space lets a reference break across a line, and one of them was doing exactly that (see below). The converter reused `check_xrefs.py`'s own REF/QTY/PROT predicate, so the conversion could not disagree with the checker about what it was converting; LaTeX commands were masked to equal-length filler first, keeping offsets true so a number inside `\label{sec:10.3.2}` or an `\autocite` key stayed invisible. **This pass edited prose, so the proof is stronger than D-065's:** the rendered book was compared against the pre-conversion PDF and **all 217 distinct references render identical numbers** (page-by-page, so cross-page adjacency could not distort the count). The whole-document word stream differs in 38 runs, every one of them reflow — running heads moving relative to body text, and four words newly hyphenating because the tie changed line breaking. **The one substantive change is a repair:** "…is covered at section" had ended page 84 with "4.2.2." beginning page 85, so the reader had to turn the page to learn which section; it now holds together. **Two gaps in the old predicate, found and fixed here:** its list-continuation pattern allowed only one number before a connector, so the third item in "chapters 3, 7 and 8" was never checked; and it only ever matched dotted numbers, so bare chapter references were outside its scope entirely. **`\chapter*` needed care:** front and back matter do not set `\@currentlabel`, so a plain `\label` after one captures whatever number was last set — silently wrong. `\unnumberedlabel` in `preamble.tex` pins the value, so the three references to chapter 0 resolve correctly. `check_xrefs.py` is rewritten for this world: it now checks that every `\ref` target exists, that every section carries a label, and that no section or chapter number is left typed into the prose. | confirmed (author's own words) |

Old 8.7.7 is now 8.7.10. Four rather than the two or three the walkthrough offered, because the heads fall into four subjects and every two- or three-way grouping left one section over 2,300 words with three run-in heads, which is the condition the split exists to remove. **The text is unchanged except three transition sentences**, each needed because a sentence that had pointed within a section now pointed across a boundary: "the rest of this section's arguments" → "the next three sections' arguments"; "Almost none of this is binding" → "Almost none of the international governance of AI is binding" (the demonstrative had lost its referent, D-055); and the closer's "This section is an account" → "Sections 8.7.6 through 8.7.9 are an account". Verified by diffing the concatenation of the four new files against the old file: those three lines, four headings, three labels, and one renumbered pointer are the whole difference. **All 24 inbound references to the old pair were read and placed by hand**, because a split manufactures D-050's failure class — a reference that resolves, to a section that no longer says what the citing sentence claims — wherever this step is skipped. | D-069 | 2026-08-25 | **Five defects from the D-062-base cloud review: two tools, two documents, three uncited bibliography entries** (extends D-067's tool-gap note) | **Author's instruction: "do the tool and doc fixes, and add the three autocites."** A cloud review over the D-062..D-067 range returned five findings; each was checked against the tree before anything was applied, and one of the five carries reasoning that does not hold. **`check_structure.py`'s environment check had never run.** Its `ENV` regex was `r"\\\\(begin|end)\{...\}"`, which compiles to a pattern requiring two literal backslashes where LaTeX writes one, so it matched nothing in any real section: `ch02/02_04.tex` contains two `\begin`/`\end` pairs and the regex returned zero matches, while synthetic `\\begin{quote}` matched. Invariant 4 -- the one `.githooks/pre-commit` advertises as "structure" -- has therefore been passing vacuously since D-065 replaced the working dialect-tag balance check with this one. | D-081 | 2026-08-28 | **The proof is a pair: the PDF and a no-frills HTML page, generated together** | **Author's instruction: "when we generate a pdf proof, do we also generate an HTML proof? It has no pages, so nothing about widows, breaks, or the shape of a page can be judged in it. The PDF remains the only page proof. **Verified in the built file rather than asserted:** 1,043,623 bytes; 1,513 anchors and 1,323 internal links with **0 broken**; 12 chapter headings, 44 section and 106 subsection headings, which is 162 and matches `sections/ORDER.tsv` exactly; all 10 boxes converted to markup and all 4 verse blocks kept their line breaks; **0 images**, so the single file is whole. **Two defects in `refs.bib` that the HTML build found and the PDF had been printing.** The first stopped the build: `kosinski2023theory` and `saxe2003people` had `\"` where a quotation mark was meant, which is LaTeX's diaeresis accent, so the References had been printing *Ëvaluating Large Language Models* and *in ẗheory of mind”̈* since those entries were written. Nothing raises an error on it -- both are valid accents -- and it was invisible until tex4ht could not set `ẗ` as text and tried to rasterize it. The second the fix exposed: **seven bibliography titles carry an internal quotation mark**, in four different notations, and biblatex wraps a title in double quotes, so all seven printed wrong -- `”`Absurd`”` with the opening mark facing the wrong way, `“”`Why Should I Trust You?`”` doubled at the front, and `“the Slovak Case””` doubled at the back. A title *ending* in `'` is worse than it looks: it meets biblatex's own closing `''` and TeX's ligature program reads the three together as `”` then `’`, printing the outer quote inside the inner one. All seven now use the literal characters `‘` and `’`, which have no ligature to form, and all seven were read in both rebuilt proofs. **This is not authoring a reference entry under standing rule 2**: the titles are the published titles and the quotes are in the originals; what changed is the notation that mis-set them. **`.gitattributes` is new**, marking both proofs `-diff -merge`. They are generated whole on every rebuild, and a megabyte of regenerated markup diffed line by line would bury the source changes in the same commit -- the cost D-068 named for the PDF and D-072 restored knowing it. Marking them undiffable is the part of that cost this repository can actually pay down. **Also corrected here, and it is a correction to my own P26 report:** a count run for this decision found that P26 orphaned **two** bibliography entries, not one. `warneken2006altruistic` went with section 5.1's rewritten opener, and the sentence it supported -- children helping and sharing before they can say why -- is not made anywhere in the book now. `p26-scope.md` and `STATE.md` said "two defects this pass caused" and now say three. **Not done, and named rather than implied.** Nothing checks that the committed proof matches the current manuscript; `pipeline.md` says to rebuild it and that remains a human rule. There is no EPUB. The page is styled with tex4ht's own stylesheet plus a font stack and three overrides, which is what "no-frills" was asked for; it has no navigation beyond the table of contents and no chapter-split alternative. **And one thing the HTML build made countable, which is now Q-025**: the book's prose writes 230 double quotes as the straight character `"`, which LuaLaTeX sets as `”` in both positions, so all 115 quotations in the book open with the mark that should close them. The rule that produced it is `style.md` section 10's, written for the plain-text dialect era and not revisited at the LaTeX migration. Split on the political-economy line it yields 5,666 words against 17,558, which leaves the remainder 1.5 times the next-largest and does not solve the problem the instruction is about; and the political-economy chapter would be one 3,327-word section, the jobs guarantee D-043 rebuilt, with five short sections around it — the shape D-092 had just removed from chapter 9. **The mass is in section 8.7**, 8,179 words in 11 files about states and borders, longer than five of the book's chapters, and D-067's record already called the structure it hid "a chapter's worth." **Checked before cutting:** 8.6 and 8.7 are separable, with exactly one `\ref` crossing between the two trees and none the other way, so the sentence describing chapter 8's "central case for a gap in accountability law" is not evidence of one argument spanning both; and the chapter's bulk is not survey, since 8.2.2 carries the aggregation limit that is the book's headline claim and 8.4.2 answers *Stochastic Parrots* against this book's own opportunity cost — a compression pass would have been the wrong instrument. **Three cuts at existing boundaries, no reordering:** chapter 8 keeps 8.1–8.3 with their numbers and levels unchanged and is retitled *Coordination, the Public, and the Political Economy* (8,128 words); old 8.4–8.6 become 9.1–9.3 as *Policy, Law, and Accountability*, promoting nothing (6,817); old 8.7 becomes chapter 10, *Governance Across Borders* (8,013). Chapters 9, 10 and 11 shift to 11, 12 and 13. `renumber-map_2026-08-28c.tsv` is the map; **189 `\ref` repointed**, 44 files moved, 11 headings promoted, 162 sections. **The old opener's claim that "the coordination and the policy belong in one chapter" is what the split retires**, and what it protected is kept as an argument rather than as a structural justification: a well-designed system built inside institutions with no reason to demand one is a design nobody has to use. Chapter 9's opener is new and carries section 8.2.2's aggregation limit forward as the thing chapter 9 is asked to supply. **Twenty-one whole-chapter references were read and placed by hand**; six were ranges, two still point at chapter 8, and thirteen moved to what their sentence actually claims. All four "this chapter" phrases inside the old chapter survive, and 8.2.2's improves: the machinery it says the chapter lacks is now visibly in chapters 9 and 10. **The cap is met: 22,958 against 23,000**, a 512-word net reduction that also absorbs 174 words of new chapter-9 opener.
